1→"""Batch execution parser option group for review command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→
     8→def _add_batch_execution_options(p_review: argparse.ArgumentParser) -> None:
     9→    g_batch = p_review.add_argument_group("batch execution")
    10→    g_batch.add_argument(
    11→        "--run-batches",
    12→        action="store_true",
    13→        help="Run holistic investigation batches with subagents and merge/import output",
    14→    )
    15→    g_batch.add_argument(
    16→        "--runner",
    17→        choices=["codex"],
    18→        default="codex",
    19→        help="Subagent runner backend (default: codex)",
    20→    )
    21→    g_batch.add_argument(
    22→        "--parallel", action="store_true", help="Run selected batches in parallel"
    23→    )
    24→    g_batch.add_argument(
    25→        "--max-parallel-batches",
    26→        type=int,
    27→        default=3,
    28→        help=(
    29→            "Max concurrent subagent batches when --parallel is enabled "
    30→            "(default: 3)"
    31→        ),
    32→    )
    33→    g_batch.add_argument(
    34→        "--batch-timeout-seconds",
    35→        type=int,
    36→        default=20 * 60,
    37→        help="Per-batch runner timeout in seconds (default: 1200)",
    38→    )
    39→    g_batch.add_argument(
    40→        "--batch-max-retries",
    41→        type=int,
    42→        default=1,
    43→        help=(
    44→            "Retries per failed batch for transient runner/network errors "
    45→            "(default: 1)"
    46→        ),
    47→    )
    48→    g_batch.add_argument(
    49→        "--batch-retry-backoff-seconds",
    50→        type=float,
    51→        default=2.0,
    52→        help=(
    53→            "Base backoff delay for transient batch retries in seconds "

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive summary document with exact file paths, line numbers, and code snippets:

## Summary for Issue #394: `--scan-after-import` Flag and Mid-Cycle Scan Guard

Based on my analysis of the codebase, here are the exact locations and implementation details:

### 1. Flag Definition

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin_review_options_batch.py`
**Lines**: 110-114

```python
g_batch.add_argument(
    "--scan-after-import",
    action="store_true",
    help="Run `scan` after successful merged import",
)
```

### 2. Flag Extraction in ReviewOptions

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/cmd.py`
**Lines**: 23-61

The `ReviewOptions` dataclass extracts the flag:
- Line 36: `scan_after_import: bool = False`
- Line 55: `scan_after_import=bool(getattr(args, "scan_after_import", False))`

The flag is passed to three entry points:
- Line 155: `do_import_run()` (for `--import-run` mode)
- Line 178: `do_external_submit()` (for `--external-submit` mode)

### 3. Mid-Cycle Scan Guard Implementation

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/preflight.py`

The guard is implemented in the `review_rerun_preflight()` function (lines 212-278):

**Key Guard Logic**:
- Lines 241-266: Checks for open backlog (objective issues + subjective queue items)
- Line 251-254: Counts backlog using `queue_context()` and validates that both objective and subjective counts are zero
- Line 262-266: Raises `CommandError` if backlog exists
- Line 232-239: Can be bypassed with `--force-review-rerun` flag

**Backlog Calculation**:
- Lines 123-150 in `_objective_and_subjective_backlog()`: 
  - Objective count comes from `ctx.snapshot.planned_objective_count`
  - Subjective count comes from filtering `ctx.snapshot.all_postflight_review_items` by dimension

This preflight is called before:
- Default prepare mode (line 212 in cmd.py)
- `--run-batches` mode (line 138 in cmd.py)

### 4. Scan Invocation After Import

**Three locations where `--scan-after-import` triggers a scan**:

#### A. `do_import_run()` - For `--import-run` mode
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py`
**Lines**: 593-608

```python
if scan_after_import and not dry_run:
    lang_name = getattr(lang, "name", None) or str(getattr(lang, "lang", ""))
    if lang_name:
        run_followup_scan(
            lang_name=lang_name,
            scan_path=scan_path,
            deps=FollowupScanDeps(
                project_root=_runtime_project_root(),
                timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
                python_executable=sys.executable,
                subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
                timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
                colorize_fn=colorize,
            ),
        )
```

#### B. `do_external_submit()` - For `--external-submit` mode
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py`
**Lines**: 553-567

```python
if scan_after_import:
    code = run_followup_scan(
        lang_name=lang.name,
        scan_path=scan_path,
        deps=FollowupScanDeps(
            project_root=_runtime_project_root(),
            timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
            python_executable=sys.executable,
            subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
            timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
            colorize_fn=colorize,
        ),
    )
    if code != 0:
        raise CommandError(f"External review exited with code {code}", exit_code=code)
```

#### C. `import_and_finalize()` - For `--run-batches` mode
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution_results.py`
**Lines**: 213-222

```python
if getattr(args, "scan_after_import", False):
    followup_code = run_followup_scan_fn(
        lang_name=lang.name,
        scan_path=str(args.path),
    )
    if followup_code != 0:
        raise CommandError(
            f"Error: follow-up scan failed with exit code {followup_code}.",
            exit_code=followup_code,
        )
```

### 5. Key Insight: The Guard Gap

The mid-cycle scan guard (`review_rerun_preflight()`) is called **before** `--run-batches` and the default prepare mode, but it is **NOT called** for:
- `--import-run` mode (calls `do_import_run()` directly)
- `--external-submit` mode (calls `do_external_submit()` directly)

This means the `--scan-after-import` flag can trigger a scan **after import even when the queue has remaining items**, because these import modes bypass the preflight guard.

To bypass the guard in `--run-batches` mode, use `--force-review-rerun` (line 233-239 in preflight.py).

commit 3e446536552e34a6e0e34dc6811a299137579c4e
Author: POM <[REDACTED]>
Date:   Fri Feb 20 15:24:42 2026 +0100

    Fix issue #127: review --prepare uses last scan_path; smells fixer checks actual findings
    
    Bug 1: `desloppify review --prepare` reported "0 files in codebase" when the
    project had TypeScript files at the root rather than under src/. Root cause:
    _resolve_default_path fell back to `lang.default_src` ("src") when --path was
    omitted, ignoring the scan_path already saved in state. Fix: for the review
    command, load state first and use scan_path as the default path.
    
    Bug 2: scan repeatedly suggested `desloppify fix dead-useeffect --dry-run` on
    a Node backend with no React code, always returning 0 candidates. Root cause:
    _append_auto_fix_actions blindly picked the first registered fixer for the smells
    detector regardless of whether matching open findings existed. Fix: add
    _fixer_has_applicable_findings() which checks detail.smell_id on open findings
    before including a fixer in the candidate list.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>

 4 files changed, 237 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)

```
def _resolve_handler(command: str):
    return COMMAND_HANDLERS[command]


def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
    try:
        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
    except SystemExit:
        return


def main() -> None:
    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
            try:
                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
            except (AttributeError, OSError):
                logger.debug(
                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
                )

    parser = create_parser()
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.command == "help":
        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
        return

    try:
        with runtime_scope():
            _resolve_default_path(args)
            _load_shared_runtime(args)

            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
            handler(args)
    except LangResolutionError as exc:
        from desloppify.utils import colorize

        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted.")
        sys.exit(1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

diff --git a/desloppify/cli.py b/desloppify/cli.py
index 620fd7d1..3cccd7fe 100644
--- a/desloppify/cli.py
+++ b/desloppify/cli.py
@@ -106,6 +106,23 @@ def _apply_persisted_exclusions(
     )
 
 
+def _project_root_from_state_path(state_path_value: str | Path | None) -> Path | None:
+    """Infer project root from an explicit state path under ``.desloppify``."""
+    if state_path_value in (None, ""):
+        return None
+    try:
+        state_file = Path(state_path_value).resolve()
+    except OSError:
+        return None

### Conclusion:

The fix has been **fully implemented** as of commit `5c23c631` (March 12, 2026). The `_project_root_from_state_path()` function and its integration into the CLI flow address the issue by:
1. Extracting project root from an explicit `--state` argument

3. Allowing `_resolve_default_path()` to correctly locate and use the saved `scan_path` from state files

158→def run_followup_scan(
   159→    *,
   160→    lang_name: str,
   161→    scan_path: str,
   162→    deps: FollowupScanDeps,
   163→    force_queue_bypass: bool = False,
   164→) -> int:
   165→    """Run a follow-up scan and return a non-zero status when it fails."""
   166→    scan_cmd = [
   167→        deps.python_executable,
   168→        "-m",
   169→        "desloppify",
   170→        "--lang",
   171→        lang_name,
   172→        "scan",
   173→        "--path",
   174→        scan_path,
   175→    ]
   176→    if force_queue_bypass:
   177→        followup_attest = (
   178→            "I understand this is not the intended workflow and "
   179→            "I am intentionally skipping queue completion"
   180→        )
   181→        scan_cmd.extend(["--force-rescan", "--attest", followup_attest])
   182→        print(
   183→            deps.colorize_fn(
   184→                "  Follow-up scan queue bypass enabled (--force-followup-scan).",
   185→                "yellow",
   186→            )
   187→        )
   188→    print(deps.colorize_fn("\n  Running follow-up scan...", "bold"))
   189→    try:
   190→        result = deps.subprocess_run(
   191→            scan_cmd,
   192→            cwd=str(deps.project_root),
   193→            timeout=deps.timeout_seconds,
   194→        )
   195→    except deps.timeout_error:
   196→        print(
   197→            deps.colorize_fn(
   198→                f"  Follow-up scan timed out after {deps.timeout_seconds}s.",
   199→                "yellow",
   200→            ),
   201→            file=sys.stderr,
   202→        )
   203→        return 124
   204→    except OSError as exc:
   205→        print(
   206→            deps.colorize_fn(f"  Follow-up scan failed: {exc}", "red"),
   207→            file=sys.stderr,
   208→        )
   209→        return 1
   210→    return int(getattr(result, "returncode", 0) or 0)
   211→
   212→

462→def _is_mid_cycle_scan(plan: dict[str, object], state: state_mod.StateModel) -> bool:
   463→    """Return True when a plan cycle is active and queue items remain.
   464→
   465→    Extends ``is_mid_cycle`` (which checks ``plan_start_scores``) with an
   466→    additional queue-items guard — even if a cycle is nominally active, we
   467→    only skip destructive operations when work actually remains.
   468→
   469→    Mid-cycle scans (via --force-rescan or PHASE_TRANSITION gate) must NOT
   470→    regenerate clusters or inject triage stages — doing so wipes triage
   471→    state and reorders the queue, undoing prioritisation work.
   472→    """
   473→    if not is_mid_cycle(plan):
   474→        return False
   475→    order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
   476→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   477→    return any(item not in skipped for item in order)
   478→
   479→
   480→def _sync_post_scan_with_policy(
   481→    *,
   482→    plan: dict[str, object],
   483→    state: state_mod.StateModel,
   484→    target_strict: float,
   485→    policy,
   486→    cycle_just_completed: bool,
   487→    force_rescan: bool = False,
   488→) -> bool:
   489→    """Run post-scan sync steps that require policy/cycle context.
   490→
   491→    When running mid-cycle (plan_start_scores set, queue non-empty) or
   492→    via --force-rescan, skip auto-clustering and triage injection.  These
   493→    steps regenerate queue structure and issue IDs, which wipes triage
   494→    state and reorders the queue.  They only run at cycle boundaries
   495→    (pre-flight / post-flight).
   496→    """
   497→    dirty = False
   498→    mid_cycle = _is_mid_cycle_scan(plan, state) or force_rescan
   499→
   500→    if _sync_subjective_and_log(
   501→        plan,
   502→        state,
   503→        policy=policy,
   504→        cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed,
   505→    ):
   506→        dirty = True
   507→    if not mid_cycle:
   508→        if _sync_auto_clusters_and_log(
   509→            plan,
   510→            state,
   511→            target_strict=target_strict,
   512→            policy=policy,
   513→        ):
   514→            dirty = True
   515→    else:
   516→        print(
   517→            colorize(
   518→                "  Plan: mid-cycle scan — skipping cluster regeneration to "
   519→                "preserve queue state.",
   520→                "dim",
   521→            )
```

   173→    *,

1→"""Scan preflight guard: warn and gate scan when queue has unfinished items."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import (
     9→    ScoreDisplayMode,
    10→    plan_aware_queue_breakdown,
    11→    score_display_mode,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import get_plan_start_strict
    14→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    15→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
    16→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    17→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.context import resolve_plan_load_status
    18→
    19→_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→
    22→def scan_queue_preflight(args: object) -> None:
    23→    """Warn and gate scan when queue has unfinished items."""
    24→    # CI profile always passes
    25→    if getattr(args, "profile", None) == "ci":
    26→        return
    27→
    28→    # --force-rescan with valid attestation bypasses
    29→    if getattr(args, "force_rescan", False):
    30→        attest = getattr(args, "attest", None) or ""
    31→        if "i understand" not in attest.lower():
    32→            raise CommandError(
    33→                '--force-rescan requires --attest "I understand this is not '
    34→                "the intended workflow and I am intentionally skipping queue "
    35→                'completion"'
    36→            )
    37→        print(
    38→            colorize(
    39→                "  --force-rescan: bypassing queue completion check. "
    40→                "Queue-destructive reconciliation steps will be skipped.",
    41→                "yellow",
    42→            )
    43→        )
    44→        return
    45→
    46→    # No plan = no gate (first scan, or user never uses plan). Use the same
    47→    # plan-load contract as the work queue so degraded-plan handling stays
    48→    # consistent across queue rendering and scan preflight.
    49→    plan_status = resolve_plan_load_status()
    50→    if plan_status.degraded:
    51→        _logger.debug(
    52→            "scan preflight plan load degraded: %s",
    53→            plan_status.error_kind,
    54→        )
    55→        return
    56→    plan = plan_status.plan
    57→    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
    58→        return
    59→    if not plan.get("plan_start_scores"):
    60→        return  # No active cycle
    61→
    62→    # Count plan-aware remaining items.  Block scan when ANY queue items
    63→    # remain (objective OR subjective).  Mid-cycle scans regenerate issue
    64→    # IDs which wipes triage state and re-clusters the queue, undoing
    65→    # prioritisation work.
    66→    try:
    67→        state = state_mod.load_state(state_path(args))
    68→        breakdown = plan_aware_queue_breakdown(state, plan)
    69→        plan_start_strict = get_plan_start_strict(plan)
    70→        mode = score_display_mode(breakdown, plan_start_strict)
    71→    except OSError:
    72→        _logger.debug("scan preflight queue breakdown skipped", exc_info=True)
    73→        return
    74→    if mode is ScoreDisplayMode.LIVE:
    75→        return  # Queue fully clear or no active cycle — scan allowed
    76→
    77→    remaining = breakdown.queue_total
    78→    # GATE — block both FROZEN (objective work) and PHASE_TRANSITION
    79→    # (subjective/workflow items remain)
    80→    raise CommandError(
    81→        f"{remaining} item{'s' if remaining != 1 else ''}"
    82→        " remaining in your queue.\n"
    83→        "  Scanning mid-cycle regenerates issue IDs and breaks triage state.\n"
    84→        "  Work through items with `desloppify next`, then scan when clear.\n\n"
    85→        "  To force a rescan (resets your plan-start score):\n"
    86→        '    desloppify scan --force-rescan --attest "I understand this is not '
    87→        "the intended workflow and I am intentionally skipping queue "
    88→        'completion"'
    89→    )
    90→
    91→
    92→__all__ = ["scan_queue_preflight"]
    93→
```

1→"""Shared subprocess runner helpers for codex batch execution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import shutil
     7→import sys
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.review.runner_process_impl.attempts import (
    11→    handle_early_attempt_return,
    12→    handle_failed_attempt,
    13→    handle_successful_attempt,
    14→    handle_timeout_or_stall,
    15→    resolve_retry_config,
    16→    run_batch_attempt,

    20→    CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    21→    FollowupScanDeps,
    22→)
    23→
    24→
    25→def _resolve_executable(name: str) -> list[str]:
    26→    """Resolve an executable, handling Windows .cmd/.bat wrappers.
    27→
    28→    On Windows, npm-installed CLIs are ``.cmd`` batch scripts that cannot be
    29→    executed directly by ``subprocess`` without ``shell=True``.  Prefixing
    30→    with ``cmd /c`` avoids needing ``shell=True`` while still resolving them.
    31→
    32→    When ``shutil.which()`` cannot locate the executable on Windows, we still
    33→    route through ``cmd /c`` so the shell's own PATH resolution can find
    34→    ``.cmd``/``.bat`` wrappers that Python's ``which`` missed.
    35→    """
    36→    resolved = shutil.which(name)
    37→    if sys.platform == "win32":
    38→        if resolved is not None and resolved.lower().endswith((".cmd", ".bat")):
    39→            return ["cmd", "/c", resolved]
    40→        # shutil.which may miss .cmd/.bat wrappers — let cmd.exe resolve it
    41→        return ["cmd", "/c", resolved or name]
    42→    return [resolved or name]
    43→
    44→
    45→def codex_batch_command(*, prompt: str, repo_root: Path, output_file: Path) -> list[str]:
    46→    """Build one codex exec command line for a batch prompt."""
    47→    effort = os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT", "low").strip().lower()
    48→    if effort not in {"low", "medium", "high", "xhigh"}:
    49→        effort = "low"
    50→    prefix = _resolve_executable("codex")
    51→    return [
    52→        *prefix,
    53→        "exec",
    54→        "--ephemeral",
    55→        "-C",
    56→        str(repo_root),
    57→        "-s",
    58→        "workspace-write",
    59→        "-c",
    60→        'approval_policy="never"',
    61→        "-c",
    62→        f'model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"',
    63→        "-o",
    64→        str(output_file),
    65→        prompt,
    66→    ]
    67→
    68→
    69→def run_codex_batch(
    70→    *,
    71→    prompt: str,
    72→    repo_root: Path,
    73→    output_file: Path,
    74→    log_file: Path,
    75→    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    76→    codex_batch_command_fn=None,
    77→) -> int:
    78→    """Execute one codex batch and return a stable CLI-style status code."""
    79→    if codex_batch_command_fn is None:
    80→        codex_batch_command_fn = codex_batch_command
    81→    cmd = codex_batch_command_fn(
    82→        prompt=prompt,
    83→        repo_root=repo_root,
    84→        output_file=output_file,
    85→    )
    86→    config = resolve_retry_config(deps)
    87→    log_sections: list[str] = []
    88→
    89→    for attempt in range(1, config.max_attempts + 1):
    90→        header, result = run_batch_attempt(
    91→            cmd=cmd,
    92→            deps=deps,
    93→            output_file=output_file,
    94→            log_file=log_file,
    95→            log_sections=log_sections,
    96→            attempt=attempt,
    97→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
    98→            use_popen=config.use_popen,
    99→            live_log_interval=config.live_log_interval,
   100→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
   101→        )
   102→        early_return = handle_early_attempt_return(result)
   103→        if early_return is not None:
   104→            return early_return
   105→        timeout_or_stall = handle_timeout_or_stall(
   106→            header=header,
   107→            result=result,
   108→            deps=deps,
   109→            output_file=output_file,
   110→            log_file=log_file,
   111→            log_sections=log_sections,
   112→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
   113→        )
   114→        if timeout_or_stall is not None:
   115→            if timeout_or_stall == 0:
   116→                return 0  # recovered from timeout/stall
   117→            # Non-recovered timeout/stall: retry if attempts remain
   118→            if attempt < config.max_attempts:
   119→                delay = config.retry_backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1))
   120→                log_sections.append(
   121→                    f"Timeout/stall on attempt {attempt}/{config.max_attempts}; "
   122→                    f"retrying in {delay:.1f}s."
   123→                )
   124→                if delay > 0:
   125→                    deps.sleep_fn(delay)
   126→                continue
   127→            return timeout_or_stall
   128→
   129→        log_sections.append(
   130→            f"{header}\n\nSTDOUT:\n{result.stdout_text}\n\nSTDERR:\n{result.stderr_text}\n"
   131→        )
   132→
   133→        success_code = handle_successful_attempt(
   134→            result=result,
   135→            output_file=output_file,
   136→            log_file=log_file,
   137→            deps=deps,
   138→            log_sections=log_sections,
   139→        )
   140→        if success_code is not None:
   141→            return success_code
   142→        failure_code = handle_failed_attempt(
   143→            result=result,
   144→            deps=deps,
   145→            attempt=attempt,
   146→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
   147→            retry_backoff_seconds=config.retry_backoff_seconds,
   148→            log_file=log_file,
   149→            log_sections=log_sections,
   150→        )
   151→        if failure_code is not None:
   152→            return failure_code
   153→
   154→    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
   155→    return 1
   156→

## Summary: Issue #394 - `--scan-after-import` Blocked by Mid-Cycle Scan Guard

I've found the complete picture of the issue. Here's the exact file paths, line numbers, and guard logic:

### 1. **`run_followup_scan` Function**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/runner/codex_batch.py`
**Lines:** 158-210

```python
def run_followup_scan(
    *,
    lang_name: str,
    scan_path: str,
    deps: FollowupScanDeps,
    force_queue_bypass: bool = False,
) -> int:
    """Run a follow-up scan and return a non-zero status when it fails."""
    scan_cmd = [
        deps.python_executable,
        "-m",
        "desloppify",
        "--lang",
        lang_name,
        "scan",
        "--path",
        scan_path,
    ]
    if force_queue_bypass:
        # Adds --force-rescan and --attest to bypass the guard
        followup_attest = (
            "I understand this is not the intended workflow and "
            "I am intentionally skipping queue completion"
        )
        scan_cmd.extend(["--force-rescan", "--attest", followup_attest])
        print(...)
    # ... subprocess.run the scan_cmd
```

**How it invokes scan:** It shells out to `desloppify scan --path <path>` as a subprocess (line 190-194).

**The `force_queue_bypass` parameter** (line 163) is currently **NOT being passed** when `run_followup_scan` is called from `--scan-after-import` workflows. This is the root cause of issue #394.

---

### 2. **Mid-Cycle Scan Guard (Preflight)**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/preflight.py`
**Lines:** 22-89

**Guard function:** `scan_queue_preflight(args)` (line 22)

**Where it blocks:** In the `scan` command at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/cmd.py`, line 116:
```python
def cmd_scan(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Run all detectors, update persistent state, show diff."""
    scan_preflight_mod.scan_queue_preflight(args)  # <-- CALLED FIRST, before anything else
```

**The guard logic:**
1. **Lines 25-26:** Skip gate if `profile == "ci"`
2. **Lines 29-44:** **Bypass logic** — if `--force-rescan` flag is present AND `--attest` contains "i understand", skip the gate and allow the scan
3. **Lines 46-75:** If no plan or no cycle, allow scan
4. **Lines 77-89:** **BLOCK** — if queue has remaining items and no `--force-rescan` flag, raise `CommandError` with message:
   ```
   {remaining} item(s) remaining in your queue.
   Scanning mid-cycle regenerates issue IDs and breaks triage state.
   ```

---

### 3. **Current `--force-rescan` Flag**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups.py`
**Lines:** 100-109

The flag **DOES exist** and is documented. When passed with the proper attestation, it bypasses the preflight guard.

---

### 4. **The Problem: `force_queue_bypass` Not Wired Through**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py`
**Lines:** 156-167

The function that wraps the call:
```python
def _run_followup_scan_with_deps(
    *,
    lang_name,
    scan_path,
    deps: FollowupScanDeps,
) -> int:
    """Run a post-import scan with prebuilt scan deps."""
    return run_followup_scan(
        lang_name=lang_name,
        scan_path=scan_path,
        deps=deps,
        # NOTE: force_queue_bypass is NOT passed here!
    )
```

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution_results.py`
**Lines:** 213-222

Where `--scan-after-import` triggers the followup:
```python
if getattr(args, "scan_after_import", False):
    followup_code = run_followup_scan_fn(
        lang_name=lang.name,
        scan_path=str(args.path),
        # NOTE: No way to pass force_queue_bypass here!
    )
```

---

### 5. **Test Coverage**
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_runner_modules_direct.py`
**Lines:** 88-113

Shows the intended usage:

## Summary of Findings

| Aspect | Details |
|--------|---------|
| **`run_followup_scan` location** | `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/runner/codex_batch.py:158-210` |
| **How it invokes scan** | Shells out: `python -m desloppify --lang <lang> scan --path <path>` |
| **Bypass parameter** | `force_queue_bypass: bool = False` (line 163) — when True, adds `--force-rescan --attest` to command |
| **Mid-cycle guard location** | `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/preflight.py:22-89` |
| **Guard entry point** | `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/cmd.py:116` |
| **Guard blocks when** | Queue has remaining items AND `--force-rescan` not passed with valid attestation |
| **Existing `--force-rescan` flag** | YES — exists at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups.py:100-109` |
| **The bug** | `force_queue_bypass` param is **never passed** from `--scan-after-import` workflows; it defaults to False, so the subprocess scan hits the preflight guard |
| **Call chain gap** | `execution_results.py:213` → `run_followup_scan_fn()` → `orchestrator.py:163` → `run_followup_scan()` — no `force_queue_bypass=True` propagated through the chain |

4. **Shared framework changes**: The PR touches some shared files — are those changes safe and backward-compatible?

5. **Red flags**: Any security issues, hardcoded paths, or things that would break on different platforms?

Don't nitpick style — just check for structural soundness. Give me a summary of whether it's good to merge and any concerns.

{"additions":7296,"body":"## Why\n\nRust support was still shallow compared with the existing full plugins.\n\nThis PR brings Rust up to full-plugin depth so Rust repositories get:\n\n- Rust-specific detector phases instead of generic coverage only\n- official toolchain-backed checks through `cargo clippy`, `cargo check`, and `cargo rustdoc`\n- Rust-aware dependency graphing and test coverage mapping\n- Rust-specific smell detection, review guidance, and targeted fixers\n\n## What changed\n\n### Full Rust plugin\n\n- added full Rust plugin registration and phase wiring in `desloppify/languages/rust/__init__.py`\n- added Rust detect-command registry in `desloppify/languages/rust/commands.py`\n- added Rust extractor, support, tool, review, test coverage, and fixer modules\n- added maintainer documentation in `desloppify/languages/rust/README.md`\n\n### Rust detectors\n\n- split Rust detectors into focused modules:\n  - `api.py`\n  - `cargo_policy.py`\n  - `safety.py`\n  - `smells.py`\n  - `smells_catalog.py`\n  - `_shared.py`\n  - `deps.py`\n- kept `detectors/custom.py` as a compatibility shim for older imports\n\n### Rust analysis coverage\n\n- added Rust policy detectors for:\n  - same-crate import hygiene\n  - Cargo feature hygiene\n  - doctest / README harness hygiene\n  - API naming and boundary conventions\n  - future-proofing checks for public API shapes\n  - thread-safety contracts\n  - async locking hazards\n  - drop safety\n  - unsafe API usage\n- added Rust smell detection with a conservative catalog plus custom suppression-aware smells\n- added Rust dependency graph resolution from `mod` and `use` relationships\n- added Rust-aware test coverage heuristics for inline tests, integration tests, barrel re-exports, and runtime entrypoints\n\n### Tool-backed phases\n\n- added Cargo-based detector phases for:\n  - Clippy warnings\n  - Cargo compiler errors\n  - rustdoc warnings\n\n### Fixers\n\n- added fixers for:\n  - rewriting same-crate imports to `crate::...`\n  - inserting missing Cargo feature declarations\n  - adding README doctest harnesses\n\n### Shared framework updates\n\n- updated registry/catalog metadata for the Rust plugin\n- updated shared test-coverage plumbing where Rust-specific behavior needed framework support\n- updated common plugin tests and coverage tests to account for the Rust plugin\n\n## Validation\n\nAutomated:\n\n- `pytest -q desloppify/languages/rust/tests`\n  - `96 passed`\n- `pytest -q desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_generic_plugin.py desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_lang_init.py desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_treesitter.py desloppify/tests/detectors/coverage/test_test_coverage.py desloppify/tests/detectors/coverage/test_test_coverage_heuristics.py`\n  - `159 passed, 93 skipped`\n\nManual / external:\n\n- validated and tuned the Rust smell and unsafe-related detectors against:\n  - `tokio-rs/bytes`\n  - `serde-rs/json`\n- manually reviewed reported issues during detector tuning to reduce false positives before landing the current ruleset\n\n## Reviewer focus\n\n- phase ordering and config wiring in `desloppify/languages/rust/__init__.py`\n- detector ownership split across `api.py`, `cargo_policy.py`, `safety.py`, and `smells.py`\n- Rust comment stripping and smell suppression logic in `support.py` and `smells.py`\n- shared test-coverage changes required to support Rust mapping\n\n## Notes\n\n- this is a large feature PR because it adds a full Rust plugin rather than a small detector tweak\n- the Rust smell catalog is intentionally conservative; noisier regex-only rules were tested and removed instead of shipping false positives\n- `cargo rustdoc` currently runs with `--lib`, so binary-only rustdoc coverage remains limited by design\n- no migration steps are required for existing     49→    module_patterns as ts_review_module_patterns,
    50→)
    51→from desloppify.languages.typescript._zones import TS_ZONE_RULES
    52→from desloppify.languages.typescript.plugin_contract import (
    53→    TS_BARREL_NAMES,
    54→    TS_COMPLEXITY_THRESHOLD,
    55→    TS_DEFAULT_SRC,
    56→    TS_ENTRY_PATTERNS,
    57→    TS_EXCLUSIONS,
    58→    TS_EXTENSIONS,
    59→    TS_LARGE_THRESHOLD,
    60→)
    61→
    62→
    63→def _ts_treesitter_phases() -> list[DetectorPhase]:
    64→    """Cherry-pick tree-sitter phases that complement TS's own detectors."""
    65→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import get_spec, is_available
    66→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter.phases import make_cohesion_phase
    67→
    68→    if not is_available():
    69→        return []
    70→
    71→    spec = get_spec("typescript")
    72→    if spec is None:
    73→        return []
    74→
    75→    return [make_cohesion_phase(spec)]
    76→
    77→
    78→def _ts_extract_functions(path):
    79→    """Extract all TS functions for duplicate detection."""
    80→    from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_ts_and_tsx_files
    81→
    82→    functions = []
    83→    for filepath in find_ts_and_tsx_files(path):
    84→        if "node_modules" in filepath or ".d.ts" in filepath:
    85→            continue
    86→        functions.extend(extract_ts_functions(filepath))
    87→    return functions
    88→
    89→
    90→class TypeScriptConfig(LangConfig):
    91→    def detect_lang_security_detailed(self, files, zone_map):
    92→        result = detect_ts_security(files, zone_map)
    93→        return LangSecurityResult(
    94→            entries=result.entries,
    95→            files_scanned=result.population_size,
    96→        )
    97→
    98→    def __init__(self):
    99→        super().__init__(
   100→            name="typescript",
   101→            extensions=TS_EXTENSIONS,
   102→            exclusions=TS_EXCLUSIONS,
   103→            default_src=TS_DEFAULT_SRC,
   104→            build_dep_graph=deps_detector_mod.build_dep_graph,
   105→            entry_patterns=TS_ENTRY_PATTERNS,
   106→            barrel_names=TS_BARREL_NAMES,
   107→            phases=[
   108→                DetectorPhase("Logs", phase_logs),
   109→                DetectorPhase("Unused (tsc)", phase_unused),
   110→                DetectorPhase("Dead exports", phase_exports),
   111→                DetectorPhase("Deprecated", phase_deprecated),
   112→                DetectorPhase("Structural analysis", phase_structural),
   113→                DetectorPhase(
   114→                    "Coupling + single-use + patterns + naming",
   115→                    phase_coupling,
   116→                ),
   117→                *_ts_treesitter_phases(),
   118→                detector_phase_signature(),
   119→                detector_phase_test_coverage(),
   120→                DetectorPhase("Code smells", phase_smells),
   121→                detector_phase_security(),
   122→                *shared_subjective_duplicates_tail(),
   123→            ],
   124→            fixers=get_ts_fixers(),
   125→            get_area=get_area,
   126→            detect_commands=ts_commands_mod.get_detect_commands(),
   127→            boundaries=[
   128→                BoundaryRule("shared/", "tools/", "shared→tools"),
   129→            ],
   130→            typecheck_cmd="npx tsc --noEmit",
   131→            file_finder=iter_typescript_sources,
   132→            large_threshold=TS_LARGE_THRESHOLD,
   133→            complexity_threshold=TS_COMPLEXITY_THRESHOLD,
   134→            default_scan_profile="full",
   135→            detect_markers=["package.json"],
   136→            external_test_dirs=["tests", "test", "__tests__"],
   137→            test_file_extensions=[".ts", ".tsx"],
   138→            review_module_patterns_fn=ts_review_module_patterns,
   139→            review_api_surface_fn=ts_review_api_surface,
   140→            review_guidance=TS_REVIEW_GUIDANCE,
   141→            review_low_value_pattern=TS_LOW_VALUE_PATTERN,
   142→            holistic_review_dimensions=TS_HOLISTIC_REVIEW_DIMENSIONS,
   143→            migration_pattern_pairs=TS_MIGRATION_PATTERN_PAIRS,
   144→            migration_mixed_extensions=TS_MIGRATION_MIXED_EXTENSIONS,
   145→            extract_functions=_ts_extract_functions,
   146→            zone_rules=TS_ZONE_RULES,
   147→        )
   148→
   149→
   150→def register() -> None:
   151→    """Register TypeScript language config + hooks through an explicit entrypoint."""
   152→    register_full_plugin(
   153→        "typescript",
   154→        TypeScriptConfig,
   155→        test_coverage=ts_test_coverage_hooks,
   156→    )
   157→
   158→
   159→def register_hooks() -> None:
   160→    """Register TypeScript hook modules without language-config bootstrap."""
   161→    register_lang_hooks("typescript", test_coverage=ts_test_coverage_hooks)
   162→
   163→
   164→Config = TypeScriptConfig
   165→
   166→
   167→__all__ = [
   168→    "Config",
   169→    "TS_HOLISTIC_REVIEW_DIMENSIONS",
   170→    "TS_LOW_VALUE_PATTERN",
   171→    "TS_MIGRATION_MIXED_EXTENSIONS",

   174→    "TS_ZONE_RULES",

  1236→+def _make_tool_detect_command(
  1237→+    label: str,
  1238→+    cmd: str,

  1241→+    def command(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
  1242→+        result = run_tool_result(cmd, Path(args.path), parser)
  1243→+        if result.status == "error":
  1244→+            payload = {
  1245→+                "count": 0,
  1246→+                "entries": [],
  1247→+                "status": result.status,
  1248→+                "error_kind": result.error_kind,
  1249→+                "message": result.message,
  1250→+            }
  1251→+            if getattr(args, "json", False):
  1252→+                print(json.dumps(payload, indent=2))
  1253→+                return
  1254→+            print(colorize(f"\n{label} unavailable", "yellow"))
  1255→+            if result.message:
  1256→+                print(colorize(result.message, "dim"))
  1257→+            return
  1258→+
  1259→+        entries = [
  1260→+            {
  1261→+                "file": rel(entry["file"]),
  1262→+                "line": entry["line"],
  1263→+                "message": entry["message"],
  1264→+            }
  1265→+            for entry in result.entries
  1266→+        ]
  1267→+        display_entries(
  1268→+            args,
  1269→+            entries,
  1270→+            label=label,
  1271→+            empty_msg=f"No {label} findings.",
  1272→+            columns=["File", "Line", "Message"],
  1273→+            widths=[60, 6, 80],
  1274→+            row_fn=lambda entry: [
  1275→+                entry["file"],
  1276→+                str(entry["line"]),
  1277→+                entry["message"],
  1278→+            ],
  1279→+            json_payload={"count": len(entries), "entries": entries},
  1280→+        )
  1281→+
  1282→+    command.__module__ = __name__
  1283→+    return command

  3345→+"""Rust extraction: function parsing and file discovery."""
  3346→+
  3347→+from __future__ import annotations
  3348→+
  3349→+import hashlib
  3350→+import re
  3351→+from pathlib import Path
  3352→+
  3353→+from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import resolve_path
  3354→+from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo

  3356→+    PARSE_INIT_ERRORS,
  3357→+    RUST_SPEC,
  3358→+    is_available,

  3361→+    ts_extract_functions,

  3364→+    RUST_FILE_EXCLUSIONS,

  3366→+    normalize_rust_body,

  3370→+_FUNC_DECL_RE = re.compile(
  3371→+    r"(?m)^\s*"
  3372→+    r"(?:(?:#\[[^\]]+\]\s*)*)"
  3373→+    r"(?:(?:pub(?:\([^)]*\))?|async|const|unsafe|extern\s+\"[^\"]+\")\s+)*"
  3374→+    r"fn\s+([A-Za-z_]\w*)"
  3375→+    r"(?:<[^>{}]+>)?\s*\(([^)]*)\)"
  3376→+)
  3377→+
  3378→+
  3379→+def _extract_params(raw_params: str) -> list[str]:
  3380→+    names: list[str] = []
  3381→+    for chunk in raw_params.split(","):
  3382→+        token = chunk.strip()
  3383→+        if not token:
  3384→+            continue
  3385→+        if ":" in token:
  3386→+            token = token.split(":", 1)[0].strip()
  3387→+        token = token.lstrip("&").removeprefix("mut ").strip()
  3388→+        if token == "self":
  3389→+            names.append(token)
  3390→+            continue
  3391→+        if re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z_]\w*", token):
  3392→+            names.append(token)
  3393→+    return names
  3394→+
  3395→+
  3396→+def _find_body_start(content: str, position: int) -> int | None:
  3397→+    depth = 0
  3398→+    in_string: str | None = None
  3399→+    i = position
  3400→+    while i < len(content):
  3401→+        char = content[i]
  3402→+        if in_string:
  3403→+            if char == "\\":
  3404→+                i += 2
  3405→+                continue
  3406→+            if char == in_string:
  3407→+                in_string = None
  3408→+            i += 1
  3409→+            continue
  3410→+        if char in {'"', "'"}:
  3411→+            in_string = char
  3412→+            i += 1
  3413→+            continue
  3414→+        if char == "(":
  3415→+            depth += 1
  3416→+        elif char == ")":
  3417→+            depth = max(0, depth - 1)
  3418→+        elif char == "{" and depth == 0:
  3419→+            return i
  3420→+        i += 1
  3421→+    return None
  3422→+
  3423→+
  3424→+def _find_matching_brace(content: str, open_pos: int) -> int | None:
  3425→+    depth = 0
  3426→+    in_string: str | None = None
  3427→+    i = open_pos
  3428→+    while i < len(content):
  3429→+        char = content[i]
  3430→+        if in_string:
  3431→+            if char == "\\":
  3432→+                i += 2
  3433→+                continue
  3434→+            if char == in_string:
  3435→+                in_string = None
  3436→+            i += 1
  3437→+            continue
  3438→+        if char in {'"', "'"}:
  3439→+            in_string = char
  3440→+            i += 1
  3441→+            continue
  3442→+        if char == "{":
  3443→+            depth += 1
  3444→+        elif char == "}":
  3445→+            depth -= 1
  3446→+            if depth == 0:
  3447→+                return i
  3448→+        i += 1
  3449→+    return None
  3450→+
  3451→+
  3452→+def extract_rust_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
  3453→+    """Extract Rust functions from one file using a regex fallback."""
  3454→+    content = read_text_or_none(filepath)
  3455→+    if content is None:
  3456→+        return []
  3457→+
  3458→+    functions: list[FunctionInfo] = []
  3459→+    for match in _FUNC_DECL_RE.finditer(content):
  3460→+        body_start = _find_body_start(content, match.end())
  3461→+        if body_start is None:
  3462→+            continue
  3463→+        body_end = _find_matching_brace(content, body_start)
  3464→+        if body_end is None:
  3465→+            continue
  3466→+
  3467→+        start = match.start()
  3468→+        start_line = content.count("\n", 0, start) + 1
  3469→+        end_line = content.count("\n", 0, body_end) + 1
  3470→+        body = content[start : body_end + 1]
  3471→+        normalized = normalize_rust_body(body)
  3472→+        if len(normalized.splitlines()) < 3:
  3473→+            continue
  3474→+
  3475→+        functions.append(
  3476→+            FunctionInfo(
  3477→+                name=match.group(1),
  3478→+                file=resolve_path(filepath),
  3479→+                line=start_line,
  3480→+                end_line=end_line,
  3481→+                loc=max(1, end_line - start_line + 1),
  3482→+                body=body,
  3483→+                normalized=normalized,
  3484→+                body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:12],
  3485→+                params=_extract_params(match.group(2)),
  3486→+            )
  3487→+        )
  3488→+    return functions
  3489→+
  3490→+
  3491→+def extract_functions(path: Path) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
  3492→+    """Extract all Rust functions below a directory path."""
  3493→+    file_list = find_rust_files(path)
  3494→+    if not file_list:
  3495→+        return []

  3832→+def _make_rust_tool_phase(label: str, cmd: str, parser, detector: str, tier: int):
  3833→+    def run(path: Path, lang) -> tuple[list[dict], dict[str, int]]:
  3834→+        result = run_tool_result(cmd, path, parser)
  3835→+        if result.status == "error":
  3836→+            _record_tool_failure_coverage(
  3837→+                lang,
  3838→+                detector=detector,
  3839→+                label=label,
  3840→+                result=result,
  3841→+            )
  3842→+            return [], {}
  3843→+        if not result.entries:
  3844→+            return [], {}
  3845→+        issues = [
  3846→+            make_issue(
  3847→+                detector,
  3848→+                entry["file"],
  3849→+                f"{detector}::{entry['line']}",
  3850→+                tier=tier,
  3851→+                confidence="medium",
  3852→+                summary=entry["message"],
  3853→+            )
  3854→+            for entry in result.entries
  3855→+        ]
  3856→+        return issues, {detector: len(result.entries)}
  3857→+
  3858→+    return DetectorPhase(label, run)

  7495→+        if level not in allowed_levels:
  7496→+            continue
  7497→+        span = _pick_primary_span(list(message.get("spans") or []))
  7498→+        if not span:
  7499→+            continue
  7500→+        filename = str(span.get("file_name") or "").strip()
  7501→+        line_no = span.get("line_start")
  7502→+        if not filename or not isinstance(line_no, int):
  7503→+            continue
  7504→+        code = (message.get("code") or {}).get("code") or ""
  7505→+        rendered = str(message.get("rendered") or message.get("message") or "").strip()
  7506→+        if not rendered:
  7507→+            continue
  7508→+        summary = rendered.splitlines()[0].strip()
  7509→+        if code and code not in summary:
  7510→+            summary = f"[{code}] {summary}"
  7511→+        entries.append(
  7512→+            {
  7513→+                "file": filename,
  7514→+                "line": line_no,
  7515→+                "message": summary,
  7516→+            }
  7517→+        )
  7518→+    return entries
  7519→+
  7520→+
  7521→+def _parse_json_object_line(line: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
  7522→+    """Parse one cargo JSON line, ignoring human-readable noise."""
  7523→+    if not line.startswith("{"):
  7524→+        return None
  7525→+    try:
  7526→+        data = json.loads(line)
  7527→+    except json.JSONDecodeError:
  7528→+        return None
  7529→+    return data if isinstance(data, dict) else None
  7530→+
  7531→+
  7532→+def parse_clippy_messages(output: str, scan_path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
  7533→+    """Parse cargo-clippy diagnostics, including denied warnings."""
  7534→+    return _parse_cargo_messages(output, scan_path, allowed_levels={"warning", "error"})
  7535→+
  7536→+
  7537→+def parse_cargo_errors(output: str, scan_path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
  7538→+    """Parse cargo-check compiler errors only."""
  7539→+    return _parse_cargo_messages(output, scan_path, allowed_levels={"error"})
  7540→+
  7541→+
  7542→+def parse_rustdoc_messages(output: str, scan_path: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
  7543→+    """Parse rustdoc diagnostics, including denied warnings."""
  7544→+    return _parse_cargo_messages(output, scan_path, allowed_levels={"warning", "error"})

  4139→+def _strip_rust_comments_impl(text: str, *, preserve_lines: bool) -> str:
  4140→+    """Strip Rust comments without treating Markdown backticks as string delimiters."""
  4141→+    result: list[str] = []
  4142→+    i = 0
  4143→+    in_str: str | None = None
  4144→+    while i < len(text):
  4145→+        ch = text[i]
  4146→+        if in_str:
  4147→+            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(text):
  4148→+                result.append(text[i : i + 2])
  4149→+                i += 2
  4150→+                continue
  4151→+            if ch == in_str:

  4153→+            result.append(ch)
  4154→+            i += 1
  4155→+            continue
  4156→+
  4157→+        if ch == '"':
  4158→+            in_str = ch
  4159→+            result.append(ch)
  4160→+            i += 1
  4161→+            continue
  4162→+
  4163→+        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(text):
  4164→+            next_char = text[i + 1]
  4165→+            if next_char == "/":
  4166→+                if preserve_lines:
  4167→+                    while i < len(text) and text[i] != "\n":
  4168→+                        result.append(" ")
  4169→+                        i += 1
  4170→+                else:
  4171→+                    while i < len(text) and text[i] != "\n":
  4172→+                        i += 1
  4173→+                continue
  4174→+            if next_char == "*":
  4175→+                if preserve_lines:
  4176→+                    result.extend((" ", " "))
  4177→+                i += 2
  4178→+                while i < len(text):
  4179→+                    if text[i] == "*" and i + 1 < len(text) and text[i + 1] == "/":
  4180→+                        if preserve_lines:
  4181→+                            result.extend((" ", " "))
  4182→+                        i += 2
  4183→+                        break
  4184→+                    if preserve_lines:
  4185→+                        result.append("\n" if text[i] == "\n" else " ")
  4186→+                    i += 1
  4187→+                continue
  4188→+
  4189→+        result.append(ch)
  4190→+        i += 1
  4191→+    return "".join(result)

  4224→+    """Return `mod foo;` declarations plus optional `#[path = ...]` overrides."""

  4226→+    declarations: list[tuple[str, str | None]] = []
  4227→+    attrs: list[str] = []
  4228→+    for raw_line in stripped.splitlines():
  4229→+        line = raw_line.strip()
  4230→+        if not line:
  4231→+            attrs = []
  4232→+            continue
  4233→+
  4234→+        if line.startswith("#["):
  4235→+            inline_attrs = _ATTR_RE.findall(line)
  4236→+            if inline_attrs:
  4237→+                attrs.extend(inline_attrs)
  4238→+                line = _ATTR_RE.sub("", line).strip()
  4239→+                if not line:
  4240→+                    continue
  4241→+            else:
  4242→+                attrs = []
  4243→+                continue
  4244→+
  4245→+        match = _MOD_LINE_RE.match(line)
  4246→+        if match:
  4247→+            declarations.append((match.group(1), _path_override_from_attrs(attrs)))
  4248→+        attrs = []
  4249→+    return declarations
  4250→+
  4251→+
  4252→+def iter_use_specs(content: str) -> list[str]:
  4253→+    """Return normalized Rust `use` / `pub use` specs from a file."""
  4254→+    stripped = strip_rust_comments(content)
  4255→+    specs: list[str] = []
  4256→+    for match in USE_STATEMENT_RE.finditer(stripped):
  4257→+        specs.extend(_expand_use_tree(match.group(1)))
  4258→+    return specs

  4538→+def resolve_mod_declaration(
  4539→+    module_name: str,

  4552→+    candidates: list[Path] = []

  4554→+        candidates.append(source.parent / declared_path)
  4555→+    candidates.extend((base_dir / f"{module_name}.rs", base_dir / module_name / "mod.rs"))
  4556→+    for candidate in candidates:
  4557→+        matched = _candidate_matches(candidate, production_files)
  4558→+        if matched:
  4559→+            return matched
  4560→+    return None

    31→def _register_module_entrypoint(module: object) -> None:
    32→    """Run a module-level register() entrypoint when provided."""
    33→    register_fn = getattr(module, "register", None)
    34→    if register_fn is None:
    35→        return
    36→    if not callable(register_fn):
    37→        raise TypeError("Language module register entrypoint must be callable")
    38→    register_fn()

    41→def raise_load_errors() -> None:
    42→    errors = registry_state.get_load_errors()
    43→    if not errors:
    44→        return
    45→    summaries: list[str] = []
    46→    for module_name, ex in sorted(errors.items()):
    47→        ex_type = type(ex).__name__
    48→        logger.warning(
    49→            "Language plugin %s failed to load: %s: %s",
    50→            module_name,
    51→            ex_type,
    52→            ex,
    53→        )
    54→        summaries.append(f"{module_name}: {ex_type}: {ex}")
    55→    raise ImportError("; ".join(summaries))
    56→
    57→
    58→def _report_load_errors_for_load_all() -> None:
    59→    """Emit warning diagnostics for plugin failures without aborting discovery."""
    60→    try:
    61→        raise_load_errors()
    62→    except ImportError:

87→def load_all(*, force_reload: bool = False) -> None:
    88→    """Import all language modules to trigger registration."""
    89→    if force_reload:
    90→        registry_state.clear()
    91→    elif registry_state.was_load_attempted() and registry_state.all_keys():
    92→        _report_load_errors_for_load_all()
    93→        return
    94→
    95→    # Mark load-attempted early to guard against re-entrancy: if a plugin
    96→    # import transitively triggers load_all() again (e.g. via get_lang()),
    97→    # the was_load_attempted() check above will short-circuit instead of
    98→    # re-importing partially-initialised modules.
    99→    registry_state.set_load_attempted(True)
   100→
   101→    lang_dir = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
   102→    if lang_dir.name == "_framework":
   103→        lang_dir = lang_dir.parent
   104→    base_package = __package__.rsplit(".", 1)[0]
   105→    failures: dict[str, BaseException] = {}
   106→
   107→    # Discover single-file plugins by naming convention (e.g. plugin_rust.py).
   108→    for f in sorted(lang_dir.glob("plugin_*.py")):
   109→        module_name = f".{f.stem}"
   110→        try:
   111→            module = importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
   112→            _register_module_entrypoint(module)
   113→        except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
   114→            logger.debug("Language plugin import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex)
   115→            failures[module_name] = ex
   116→
   117→    # Discover packages (e.g. lang/typescript/)
   118→    for d in sorted(lang_dir.iterdir()):
   119→        if (
   120→            d.is_dir()
   121→            and (d / "__init__.py").exists()
   122→            and not d.name.startswith("_")
   123→        ):
   124→            module_name = f".{d.name}"
   125→            try:
   126→                module = importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
   127→                _register_module_entrypoint(module)
   128→            except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
   129→                logger.debug(
   130→                    "Language package import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex
   131→                )
   132→                failures[module_name] = ex
   133→
   134→    # Discover user plugins from <active-project-root>/.desloppify/plugins/*.py
   135→    # These are arbitrary code from the scan target — require explicit opt-in
   136→    # via config key "trust_plugins": true or env DESLOPPIFY_TRUST_PLUGINS=1.
   137→    try:
   138→        user_plugin_dir = get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / "plugins"
   139→        if user_plugin_dir.is_dir():
   140→            if not _user_plugins_trusted():
   141→                logger.warning(
   142→                    "Skipping user plugins in %s — not trusted. "
   143→                    "Set trust_plugins=true in .desloppify/config.json "
   144→                    "or DESLOPPIFY_TRUST_PLUGINS=1 to allow.",
   145→                    user_plugin_dir,
   146→                )
   147→            else:
   148→                for f in sorted(user_plugin_dir.glob("*.py")):
   149→                    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
   150→                        f"desloppify_user_plugin_{f.stem}", f
   151→                    )
   152→                    if spec and spec.loader:
   153→                        try:
   154→                            mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
   155→                            spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
   156→                            _register_module_entrypoint(mod)
   157→                        except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
   158→                            logger.debug(
   159→                                "User plugin import failed for %s: %s", f.name, ex
   160→                            )
   161→                            failures[f"user:{f.name}"] = ex
   162→    except OSError as exc:
   163→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "discover user plugins", exc)
   164→
   165→    registry_state.set_load_errors(failures)
   166→    _report_load_errors_for_load_all()
   167→
   168→
   169→def reload_all() -> None:
   170→    """Force a full in-process language plugin reload."""
   171→    from desloppify.base.registry import reset_registered_detectors
   172→    from desloppify.engine._scoring.policy.core import reset_registered_scoring_policies
   173→
   174→    reset_registered_detectors()
   175→    reset_registered_scoring_policies()
   176→    load_all(force_reload=True)

> AGENT

The shared `phase_signature` would use `detect_signature_variance` without any allowlist filtering. The Rust plugin defines its own `phase_signature` in `phases.py` that filters out idiomatic Rust names (`new`, `default`, `from`, `clone`, `fmt`, etc.) before running signature variance detection. This is a legitimate and well-motivated customization.

Here is my assessment:

---

## PR #389 Sense-Check Summary

**Verdict: Good to merge.** This is a well-executed, structurally sound PR. No blockers found.

### 1. Architecture -- Follows existing patterns correctly

The Rust `__init__.py` follows the exact same pattern as Python, TypeScript, Go, C#, Dart, and GDScript full plugins:
- Subclasses `LangConfig` with all required parameters
- Defines `register()` and `register_hooks()` functions
- Sets `Config = RustConfig` alias
- Proper `__all__` list

The plugin will be auto-discovered by the framework's `load_all()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/discovery.py` since it's a directory with `__init__.py` and a `register()` entrypoint.

One deliberate deviation: the Rust plugin defines its own `phase_signature` in `phases.py` instead of using the shared `detector_phase_signature()` factory. This is well-motivated -- it filters out idiomatic Rust names (`new`, `default`, `from`, `clone`, `fmt`, etc.) before running signature variance detection. All other plugins use the shared factory which has no language-specific allowlist.

### 2. Integration -- Wiring is complete

- **Registry entries**: All 13 new detector IDs are registered in `registry_catalog_entries.py` and `registry_catalog_models.py`
- **Fixer wiring**: 3 fixers (`crate-imports`, `cargo-features`, `readme-doctests`) properly reference their detector IDs
- **Commands**: Full detect-command registry built via `compose_detect_registry()` with all standard + Rust-specific commands
- **Test coverage hooks**: Properly registered via `register_full_plugin("rust", RustConfig, test_coverage=rust_test_coverage_hooks)`
- **pyproject.toml**: Test path and package-data entries added
- **Coverage mapping**: `.rs` extension added to `_infer_lang_name()` in `mapping_imports.py`

### 3. Test coverage -- Solid

117 test functions across 12 test files covering:
- All custom detectors with positive and negative cases
- Dependency graph resolution (mod declarations, workspace crates, aliases, external crate exclusion)
- Function extraction with tree-sitter fallback
- Comment stripping and body normalization
- Test coverage hooks (inline tests, import resolution, barrel re-exports, naming conventions)
- Config wiring, zone rules, plugin registration
- Tool output parsing (clippy, cargo check, rustdoc)
- Shared framework test updates for the new plugin

No obvious gaps for the scope of this PR.

### 4. Shared framework changes -- Safe and minimal

The changes to shared code are narrow and additive:
- **`test_coverage/detector.py`**: Adds inline-test awareness. Files with `#[cfg(test)]` modules are counted as tested even without separate test files. The change is guarded behind a `has_inline_tests` hook that only fires when the language module provides one, so it's backward-compatible for all other languages.
- **`test_coverage/heuristics.py`**: New `_has_inline_tests()` function that delegates to a language hook. Same pattern as existing `_is_runtime_entrypoint()` and `_has_testable_logic()`.
- **`mapping_imports.py`**: One-line addition of `.rs` to the extension-to-lang mapping.
- **Shared test updates**: The `test_registry_standalone_threshold_count` test is updated to include the 6 new medium-threshold Rust detectors. The `test_all_builtin_langs_discoverable` test moves `rust` from the generic list to the full-plugin list.

### 5. Red flags -- None found

- No hardcoded paths, no direct subprocess calls (uses framework's `run_tool_result`)
- No security concerns -- tool commands are static strings, no user-input interpolation
- `tomllib` usage is fine (project requires Python 3.11+, already used elsewhere)
- Cross-platform: uses `Path` consistently, handles `\\` normalization in `filepath.replace("\\", "/")`
- All file reads use `errors="replace"` for encoding resilience

### Minor observations (non-blocking)

- **`_record_tool_failure_coverage` import**: The Rust `phases.py` imports this underscore-prefixed function from `_framework/generic_parts/tool_factories.py`. It's the first full plugin to use it across module boundaries. Works fine, but if Tom is amenable, the framework could expose it without the underscore prefix for clarity. Not a merge blocker.
- **`detectors/custom.py` docstring**: Says "Compatibility exports for legacy Rust detector imports" but this is a brand-new plugin with no legacy consumers. The docstring is misleading -- it's actually a convenience re-export facade used by `_fixers.py`. Cosmetic only.

A full Python plugin has 15+ hand-written detector phases that understand Python-specific patterns like mutable class variables, `lru_cache` on methods with mutable args, subprocess calls without timeouts, naive regex backtracking, and dozens more. It has auto-fixers that can rewrite imports. It has subjective review dimensions tuned for Python conventions.
   314→ 
   315→ The generic system is the right starting point for any language. Only invest in a full plugin when you need detectors that understand language idioms beyond what AST pattern matching and external linters can catch.

   321→+++ b/desloppify/languages/rust/README.md
   322→@@ -0,0 +1,456 @@
   323→+# Rust Language Module
   324→+
   325→+This document explains the Rust support in Desloppify in plain language.
   326→+
   327→+It covers:
   328→+
   329→+- What the Rust module does
   330→+- How scan phases work
   331→+- What each file in `desloppify/languages/rust/` is responsible for
   332→+- Which Rust-specific detectors exist today
   333→+- Where to add new logic safely
   334→+- Current limits and practical extension points
   335→+
   336→+If you are new to this code, start with the `RustConfig` section and then read the "Scan flow" section.
   337→+
   338→+## High-level purpose
   339→+
   340→+The Rust module is a full plugin. It does more than wrap `cargo` tools.
   341→+
   342→+Current scope includes:
   343→+
   344→+- Structural analysis
   345→+- Coupling, cycles, and orphaned module detection
   346→+- Rust-specific API and Cargo policy detectors
   347→+- `cargo clippy`, `cargo check`, and `cargo rustdoc` integration
   348→+- Rust-specific code smell detection
   349→+- Duplicate detection
   350→+- Signature analysis
   351→+- Rust-aware test coverage mapping
   352→+- Subjective review guidance for Rust API and design quality
   353→+- A small set of Rust auto-fixers
   354→+
   355→+This is not a compiler-accurate semantic model. The plugin intentionally combines:
   356→+
   357→+- official toolchain signals from Cargo / Clippy / rustdoc
   358→+- best-effort Rust source parsing
   359→+- tree-sitter support when available
   360→+- hand-written Rust detectors for policy and idiom checks
   361→+
   362→+## Module map
   363→+
   364→+Files in this folder:
   365→+
   366→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/__init__.py`
   367→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/commands.py`
   368→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/extractors.py`
   369→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/phases.py`
   370→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/phases_smells.py`
   371→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/support.py`
   372→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/tools.py`
   373→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/test_coverage.py`
   374→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/review.py`
   375→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/_fixers.py`
   376→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/detectors/`
   377→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/fixers/`
   378→+- `desloppify/languages/rust/tests/`
   379→+
   380→+### What each file does
   381→+
   382→+`__init__.py`:
   383→+
   384→+- Defines `RustConfig`
   385→+- Registers the Rust plugin through `register_full_plugin(...)`
   386→+- Wires phase order, thresholds, zone rules, entry patterns, review hooks, and fixers
   387→+
   388→+`commands.py`:
   389→+
   390→+- Builds the Rust `detect` subcommand registry
   391→+- Exposes shared commands:
   392→+  - `deps`
   393→+  - `cycles`
   394→+  - `orphaned`
   395→+  - `dupes`
   396→+  - `large`
   397→+  - `complexity`
   398→+  - `smells`
   399→+- Exposes Rust-specific commands:
   400→+  - `clippy_warning`
   401→+  - `cargo_error`
   402→+  - `rustdoc_warning`
   403→+  - `rust_import_hygiene`
   404→+  - `rust_feature_hygiene`
   405→+  - `rust_doctest`
   406→+  - `rust_api_convention`
   407→+  - `rust_error_boundary`
   408→+  - `rust_future_proofing`
   409→+  - `rust_thread_safety`
   410→+  - `rust_async_locking`
   411→+  - `rust_drop_safety`
   412→+  - `rust_unsafe_api`
   413→+
   414→+`extractors.py`:
   415→+
   416→+- Finds Rust source files while skipping `target`, `vendor`, and other non-source folders
   417→+- Extracts functions for duplicate detection
   418→+- Uses tree-sitter when available and falls back to regex plus brace tracking otherwise
   419→+
   420→+`phases.py`:
   421→+
   422→+- Defines Rust structural complexity signals
   423→+- Runs structural analysis
   424→+- Runs coupling, cycles, and orphaned detection
   425→+- Runs Rust policy detectors
   426→+- Runs Rust signature analysis
   427→+- Builds the tool-backed phases for Clippy, Cargo check, and rustdoc
   428→+
   429→+`phases_smells.py`:
   430→+
   431→+- Runs Rust-specific smell detection
   432→+- Normalizes smell entries into standard issue objects
   433→+
   434→+`support.py`:
   435→+
   436→+- Shared Rust parsing and path helpers
   437→+- Comment stripping
   438→+- `use` / `mod` parsing helpers
   439→+- workspace / manifest resolution
   440→+- module and crate-name normalization helpers
   441→+- normalization helpers reused by extractors, deps, tests, and detectors
   442→+
   443→+`tools.py`:
   444→+
   445→+- Defines the Cargo command strings used by tool-backed phases
   446→+- Parses JSON diagnostics from `cargo check`, `cargo clippy`, and `cargo rustdoc`
   447→+
   448→+`test_coverage.py`:
   449→+
   450→+- Implements Rust-specific test coverage heuristics
   451→+- Maps integration tests, inline tests, imports, and barrel re-exports back to production files
   452→+
   453→+`review.py`:
   454→+
   455→+- Defines Rust subjective review dimensions
   456→+- Supplies Rust-specific review guidance and module-pattern markers
   457→+- Summarizes public API surface for review prompts
   458→+
   459→+`_fixers.py`:
   460→+
   461→+- Implements the current Rust auto-fixers
   462→+- Fixes same-crate imports
   463→+- Adds missing Cargo features
   464→+- Adds README doctest harnesses to `src/lib.rs`
   465→+
   466→+`fixers/__init__.py`:
   467→+
   468→+- Thin package wrapper that exposes the fixer registry
   469→+
   470→+## Detector layout
   471→+
   472→+Rust detectors are split by responsibility.
   473→+
   474→+`detectors/api.py`:
   475→+
   476→+- import hygiene
   477→+- public API naming conventions
   478→+- public error boundary rules
   479→+- future-proofing rules for public API shapes
   480→+- thread-safety contract checks
   481→+
   482→+`detectors/cargo_policy.py`:
   483→+
   484→+- Cargo feature hygiene
   485→+- README / inline doctest policy
   486→+
   487→+`detectors/safety.py`:
   488→+
   489→+- async locking checks
   490→+- drop safety checks
   491→+- unsafe API usage checks
   492→+
   493→+`detectors/smells.py`:
   494→+
   495→+- Rust-specific smell orchestration
   496→+- custom smell logic for cases that need context-aware suppression
   497→+
   498→+`detectors/smells_catalog.py`:
   499→+

```
500→+- smell IDs

   508→+
